<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the infinitude assertion in Artin's primitive root conjecture: for every integer <i>a</i> that is neither −1 nor a square, there are at least $`c_a x/(\log x)^2`$ primes in $`(x,2x)`$ with primitive root <i>a</i>, for some $`c_a\gt 0`$ and every sufficiently large <i>x</i>.

- **claim-main-primitive-root** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every admissible integer $a$, there are constants $c_a>0$ and $x_a\ge2$ such that, for every real $x\ge x_a$, $$\#\{p\text{ prime}:x<p<2x,\ p\nmid a,\ \mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_p(a)=p-1\} \ge c_a\frac{x}{(\log x)^2}.$$
  - Explanation: For each admissible base there is a positive lower bound of order x over the square of log x on the number of primes in a dyadic interval having that base as a primitive root, for all large x.
- **claim-hecke-zero-free** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-hecke-zero-free)): Let $F$ be a cyclotomic number field containing $\mu_{12}$. For every finite-order Hecke character $\eta$ of $F$, the meromorphic continuation of $L_F(s,\eta)$ has no zeros in $$\operatorname{Re}s>1-10^{-6}.$$
  - Explanation: Finite-order Hecke L-functions over a cyclotomic field containing twelfth roots of unity have no zeros in a half-plane of fixed width, common to all fields and characters.
- **claim-prime-reservoir** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-prime-reservoir)): Let $M$ be a fixed positive multiple of $8$, let $c\in\{2,4\}$, and let $u$ be an integer satisfying $$(u,M)=1,\qquad c\mid u-1,\qquad \left(\frac{u-1}{c},\frac Mc\right)=1. \label{alg:progression-conditions}$$ For every sufficiently large real $x$, there are $\gg_{M,c,u}x/L^2$ distinct primes $p\in(x,2x)$ satisfying $$p\equiv u\pmod M,\qquad p-1=crQ, \qquad Q>x^{0.9}\ \text{prime}, \label{eq:controlled-predecessor}$$ where $r$ is a positive integer all of whose prime divisors lie in $$\bigl(\exp(L^{0.1}),\exp(L^{0.3})\bigr).$$
  - Explanation: In a fixed progression there are many primes whose predecessor is a small cofactor times intermediate-sized primes times one large prime.
- **claim-uniform-splitting** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-splitting)): Fix an integer $a$ with $\|a\|>1$, and put $\delta_0=10^{-6}$. For all sufficiently large real $x$ and all primes $q$ sufficiently large in terms of $a$, with $q\le\exp(L^{0.3})$, one has $$\#\{p\text{ prime}:x<p\le2x,\ p\text{ splits completely in }K_q\} \ll_a \frac{x}{q(q-1)L}+x^{1-\delta_0}. \label{eq:uniform-splitting}$$ The implied constant and the threshold for $x$ are independent of $q$. For $a=2$, every prime $q\ge5$ is allowed, with absolute constants and an absolute threshold for $x$.
  - Explanation: The count of primes in a dyadic interval splitting completely in the Kummer field is bounded uniformly in the prime q, with a power saving error term.
- **claim-splitting-test** (theorem, [source](#stmt-alg-splitting-test)): Let $a\ne0$ be an integer, and let $p,q$ be primes with $p\nmid aq$. Then $p$ splits completely in $K_q$ if and only if $$p\equiv1\pmod q,\qquad a^{(p-1)/q}\equiv1\pmod p. \label{eq:kummer-frobenius-test}$$
  - Explanation: Complete splitting in the Kummer field is equivalent to two congruence conditions on the prime.
- **claim-quadratic-progression** (construction, [source](#stmt-alg-quadratic-progression)): Let $a\in\mathbb{Z}$ be neither $-1$ nor an integer square, and set $$M=8\prod_{\substack{\ell\mid a\\\ell\text{ odd prime}}}\ell.$$ There are $c\in\{2,4\}$ and an integer $u$ satisfying <a href="#alg:progression-conditions" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="alg:progression-conditions">[alg:progression-conditions]</a> such that every prime $p\equiv u\pmod M$ satisfies $p\nmid a$ and $(a/p)=-1$.
  - Explanation: For a base that is neither minus one nor a square, a residue class exists modulo an explicit modulus whose primes make the base a quadratic nonresidue and meet the local conditions of the reservoir proposition.
- **claim-remark-powers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-auto-remark-1)): Negative squares are covered by the row $d_0=-1$ in the progression lemma. Odd perfect powers also require no separate exclusion. If $a=b^h$ with fixed odd $h>1$, every prime divisor of $h$ is eventually smaller than every prime factor of $rQ$; hence $(h,p-1)=1$ for the constructed primes. This explains directly how the controlled predecessor removes the obstruction from the fixed exponent.
  - Explanation: Negative squares and odd perfect powers need no separate treatment in the construction.
- **claim-s-lattice** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-s-lattice)): The diagonal copy of $R$ is a discrete cocompact subgroup of $F_S$, is its own annihilator for the pairing $\mathrm{e}(xy)$, and has covolume $1$. For every Bruhat–Schwartz function $f$ on $F_S$, $$\sum_{m\in R}f(m)=\sum_{\nu\in R}\widehat f(\nu).$$ If $\mathfrak a=aR$ is an exterior fractional ideal, then $$q_a=q_{\mathfrak a},\qquad (aR)^\perp=a^{-1}R,\qquad \sum_{m\in aR}f(m) =q_{\mathfrak a}^{-1}\sum_{\nu\in a^{-1}R}\widehat f(\nu).$$
  - Explanation: The S-integers form a self-dual cocompact lattice of unit covolume, giving Poisson summation with no scalar factor.
- **claim-balanced-generators** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-balanced-generators)): The pairing $H_S$ on $\mathcal V$ is nondegenerate and alternating. The map $U_S/U_S^6\to\mathcal V$ is injective, its image $E$ satisfies $E^\perp=E$, and there is an isotropic subgroup $B$ such that $\mathcal V=E\oplus B$.* *Every exterior fractional ideal $\mathfrak a$ has a generator $a$ whose class $[a]$ in $\mathcal V$ belongs to $B$. Any two such generators differ by an element of $U_S^6$. They can be chosen so that $$a/\ell_{q_{\mathfrak a}}\in\mathcal K_{\mathrm{bal}}$$ for one fixed compact subset $\mathcal K_{\mathrm{bal}}\subset F_S^1$, independent of $\mathfrak a$.
  - Explanation: The sextic Hilbert pairing is symplectic, units form a self-orthogonal subgroup with an isotropic complement, and every exterior ideal has a generator in that complement, balanced up to a compact set.
- **claim-kummer-characters** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-kummer-characters)): For $u\in R\setminus\{0\}$, the ideal function $$\chi_\bullet(u)(\mathfrak a)=\chi_{\mathfrak a}(u)$$ is induced away from $S$ and the prime divisors of $(u)R$ by a finite-order Hecke character. Its values at those prime divisors are set equal to zero. If $\mathfrak f_u$ is the conductor of the inducing primitive character, then $$\mathrm{N}\mathfrak f_u\le C_{F,S}\mathrm{N}\mathop{\mathrm{rad}}((u)R) \le C_{F,S}q_u.\label{eq:kummer-conductor}$$ The same bound, with a changed constant, holds after twisting by the fixed $\eta$. The inducing characters associated to $u$ and $v$ agree if and only if $u/v\in F^{\times6}$.* *Every character of the finite group $B$, evaluated on allowed generators, extends to a finite-order Hecke character unramified outside $S$.* *The following parametrization will index the nonzero Poisson frequencies; its character distinctness and conductor bound will be used in zero detection. Choose a complete set of representatives $\mathcal U$ modulo $U_S^6$ among the nonzero S-integers whose exterior valuations belong to $\{0,1,\ldots,5\}$, with $1\in\mathcal U$. For allowed generators $r$, the map $$(u,\mathfrak r)\longmapsto [u r^6] \quad\text{from }\mathcal U\times\{\text{exterior ideals}\} \quad\text{to }(R\setminus\{0\})/U_S^6\label{eq:frequency-decomposition}$$ is a bijection. Among $u\in\mathcal U$, only $u=1$ induces the principal character, and distinct $u$’s induce distinct characters, also after the fixed twist by $\eta$.
  - Explanation: Sextic residue symbols of an S-integer are induced by finite-order Hecke characters of controlled conductor, and the frequency parametrization is a bijection with distinct characters.
- **claim-gauss-characters** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-gauss-characters)): There are a unitary Hecke character $\Lambda$, unramified outside $S$, and a finite-order Hecke character $G$, unramified outside $S$, such that for every exterior prime $\mathfrak p$, $$\gamma_2(\mathfrak p)^3=-\Lambda(\mathfrak p),\qquad G(\mathfrak p)=\overline{\chi_{\mathfrak p}(4)}\, \gamma_3(\mathfrak p),\qquad \gamma_1(\mathfrak p)\gamma_2(\mathfrak p) =-\Lambda(\mathfrak p)G(\mathfrak p). \label{eq:gauss-corrections}$$ At each complex place the infinity type of $\Lambda$ is purely angular of exponent $1$ or $-1$, in the ordinary angular coordinate. The values $\gamma_3(\mathfrak p)$ are signs and depend multiplicatively on the class of an allowed $p$ in $B$.* *For coprime squarefree exterior ideals, $$\gamma_j(\mathfrak c_1\mathfrak c_2) =\chi_{\mathfrak c_1}(c_2)^j \chi_{\mathfrak c_2}(c_1)^j \gamma_j(\mathfrak c_1)\gamma_j(\mathfrak c_2). \label{eq:gauss-crt}$$
  - Explanation: The phases of cubic and sextic prime Gauss sums are given by a unitary and a finite-order Hecke character, with a multiplicativity rule for coprime squarefree ideals.
- **claim-reflected-second-moment** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-reflection)): Let $F$ be a fixed cyclotomic field containing $\mu_{12}$, let $S$ and the arithmetic data be as above, and fix a compact subset $\mathscr K\subset F_S^\times$. For every $\epsilon>0$, every $Z\geq2$, and all dyadic lengths $P,D\geq1$, $$\label{eq:reflection-dyadic-mass} \sum_{\substack{m\in\mathscr B_Z(\mathscr K)\\ P\leq q_{\mathfrak h(m)}<2P\\ D\leq q_{\mathfrak d(m)}<2D}} \|B_m(Z)\|^2 \ll_{F,S,\eta,\mathscr K,\epsilon} Z^\epsilon P^{1/2}\left(D+\frac{D^2P}{Z}\right).$$ In particular, $$\label{eq:reflection-total-mass} \sum_{m\in\mathscr B_Z(\mathscr K)}\|B_m(Z)\|^2 \ll_{F,S,\eta,\mathscr K,\epsilon} Z^{2M_0-1+\epsilon}.$$ The estimate is uniform if $\mathscr K$ is multiplied by an element of any fixed compact subset of the norm-one group of $F_S^\times$.
  - Explanation: The coefficient family has a second moment over balanced annular rows bounded by a power of Z, with a finer dyadic version in the powerful and squarefree parts.
- **claim-theta-input** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-theta-input)): For the fixed field $F$, there is a threefold central cover $$1\longrightarrow\mu_3 \longrightarrow\widetilde{\mathrm{PGL}}_2(\mathbb{A}_F) \longrightarrow\mathrm{PGL}_2(\mathbb{A}_F)\longrightarrow1$$ which splits over $\mathrm{PGL}_2(F)$, and an irreducible genuine automorphic representation $\Theta=\bigotimes'_v\Theta_v$ on this cover, with the following properties.* 1. **Global representation and Fourier expansion.* The cover is the quotient of the $c=2$ cover $\widetilde{\mathrm{GL}}_2^{(2)}$ by the ordinary split scalar subgroup. The inducing character of $\Theta_v$, on the center of the covering torus before this quotient, is $$\label{eq:theta-central-datum} \omega_v\!\left(\zeta\,s_v(\operatorname{diag}(x^3,y^3))s_v(zI)\right) =\iota(\zeta)\left\|x^3/y^3\right\|_v^{1/6}.$$ Here $s_v$ is the ordinary torus section. The normalized inducing norm exponents are $1/6,-1/6$. The local exceptional representation is the irreducible quotient for these exponents and the irreducible subrepresentation for their Weyl conjugates. Every $\Theta_v$ has a one-dimensional Whittaker space. The global functional $$\lambda(\varphi)= \int_{F\backslash\mathbb{A}_F}\theta_\varphi(n(x)) \overline{\psi(x)}\,\,\mathrm{d}x$$ on the automorphic realization $\varphi\mapsto\theta_\varphi$ is nonzero. The representation is spherical at all but finitely many finite places. There is a preliminary pure tensor $\varphi^\circ$ on which $\lambda$ is nonzero. Its finitely many nondistinguished finite places lie in the final fixed $S$, along with the archimedean places, the places over $6$, and the places where $\psi_v$ has nonzero conductor.* *For a pure tensor $\varphi=\varphi_S\otimes\bigotimes_{v\notin S}\varphi_v$, with the distinguished spherical vector at almost every $v\notin S$, the global Whittaker function factors as $$\label{eq:theta-normalized-factorization} \mathcal W_\varphi(g) :=\lambda(\pi(g)\varphi) =\mathcal W_{S,\varphi_S}(g_S) \prod_{v\notin S}W_{v,\varphi_v}(g_v).$$ Each exterior spherical functional is normalized at one, so only finitely many factors in this product differ from one for adelic $g$. The functional at $S$ factors at its complex places; at its finite places it may be retained as a finite sum of products of local Whittaker functionals. For a smooth automorphic vector, $$\label{eq:theta-fourier-expansion} \theta_\varphi(g)=\theta_{\varphi,N}(g) +\sum_{\nu\in F^\times}\mathcal W_\varphi(a(\nu)g),\qquad \theta_{\varphi,N}(g) =\int_{F\backslash\mathbb{A}_F}\theta_\varphi(n(x)g)\,\,\mathrm{d}x .$$ This expansion converges absolutely and locally uniformly on compact sets of $g$.* 2. **Exact lifts and exterior local values.* On the quotient the chosen lifts satisfy $$\label{eq:theta-lift-identities} \begin{gathered} n(x)a(y)=a(y)n(x/y),\qquad wa(y)w^{-1}=a(y^{-1}),\\ wn(H)=n(H^{-1})a(-H^{-2})\bar n(H^{-1}). \end{gathered}$$ In [(eq:theta-normalized-factorization)](#eq:theta-normalized-factorization) the last identity $H\ne0$. At an exterior prime, when $H^{-1}$ is integral the last factor is compact, and a compact diagonal may absorb the minus sign.* *For every exterior prime $\mathfrak p$, choose a uniformizer $\varpi$, put $q_{\mathfrak p}=\mathrm{N}\mathfrak p$, and use the compact splitting on $\mathrm{PGL}_2(\mathcal O_{\mathfrak p})$. The orientation of $\iota$, equivalently that of the Hilbert-symbol convention, can be fixed so that the scalar by which the torus cocycle acts in a genuine representation is $$\label{eq:theta-effective-cocycle} \mathcal C_{\mathfrak p}(\varpi,u)=\chi_{\mathfrak p}(u)^{-2} \qquad(u\in\mathcal O_{\mathfrak p}^{\times}).$$ The compact splitting agrees with the chosen sections on unit diagonals, Weyl elements, and integral upper and lower root elements. Define $$g_{2,\mathfrak p} =q_{\mathfrak p}^{-1/2} \sum_{x\bmod\mathfrak p} \chi_{\mathfrak p}(x)^2\psi_{\mathfrak p}(x/\varpi).$$ Then $\|g_{2,\mathfrak p}\|=1$. If $W_{\mathfrak p}$ is the spherical Whittaker function normalized by $W_{\mathfrak p}(1)=1$, then, for $u\in\mathcal O_{\mathfrak p}^{\times}$, $$\label{eq:theta-local-values} W_{\mathfrak p}(a(\varpi^e u)) =\chi_{\mathfrak p}(u)^{2e} \begin{cases} 0,&e<0,\\ q_{\mathfrak p}^{-k},&e=3k,\quad k\ge0,\\ q_{\mathfrak p}^{-k-1/2}g_{2,\mathfrak p}, &e=3k+1,\quad k\ge0,\\ 0,&e=3k+2,\quad k\ge0. \end{cases}$$ In particular a shift by three nonnegative valuations multiplies the value by $q_{\mathfrak p}^{-1}$.* 3. **Complex local behavior.* At a complex place, $\Theta_v$ is the ordinary spherical $\mathrm{PGL}_2(\mathbb{C})$ principal series with normalized characters $(\|\cdot\|_v^{1/6},\|\cdot\|_v^{-1/6})$, where $\|y\|_v$ is the square of the ordinary modulus. Its spherical Whittaker function is a nonzero constant times $$\label{eq:theta-complex-bessel} \|y\|_v^{1/2}K_{1/3}\bigl(c_v\|y\|_v^{1/2}\bigr),\qquad c_v>0.$$ The constant term of the automorphic theta function belongs to the Weyl-conjugate torus induction. In particular its dependence on a complex diagonal $a(y)$ is radial, also when the vector on its right has been acted on by a fixed compact element.
  - Explanation: A threefold cover of the projective linear group over the adeles carries an irreducible genuine automorphic theta representation with one-dimensional Whittaker spaces and explicit local values.
- **claim-theta-exterior-coefficient** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-theta-exterior-coefficient)): At every exterior prime use an allowed prime generator as the uniformizer in [prop:theta-input](#prop:theta-input). Let $A\in R\setminus\{0\}$ be an allowed generator of an integral exterior ideal $\mathfrak A$. Then $$\label{eq:theta-exterior-coefficient} \prod_{\mathfrak p\notin S}W_{\mathfrak p}(a(A)) = \begin{cases} \displaystyle \frac{\gamma_2(\mathfrak c)} {q_{\mathfrak c}^{1/2}q_{\mathfrak n}}, &\mathfrak A=\mathfrak c\mathfrak n^3,\quad \mathfrak c\ \text{squarefree},\\[2mm] 0,&\text{otherwise}. \end{cases}$$ In the first case $\mathfrak c,\mathfrak n$ are uniquely determined; they need not be coprime.
  - Explanation: The product of exterior spherical Whittaker values equals the normalized Gauss sum coefficient on ideals of the form squarefree times a cube, and vanishes otherwise.
- **claim-finite-whittaker-cutoff** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-whittaker-cutoff)): Let $S_f$ be a finite set of nonarchimedean places and let $\lambda_f\ne0$ be a Whittaker functional with character $\prod_{v\in S_f}\psi_v$ on a smooth representation of the product of the local covers. For a vector $\varphi$, put $$W_\varphi(y)=\lambda_f\!\left(\pi\bigl((a(y_v))_{v\in S_f}\bigr) \varphi\right), \qquad y\in\prod_{v\in S_f}F_v^\times.$$ Given any open subgroup $H_f$ of the product of the unit groups, there are a compact open product subgroup $U\subset H_f$ and a smooth vector $\varphi_U$ for which $$W_{\varphi_U}(y)=\mathbf{1}_U(y) \qquad\left(y\in\prod_{v\in S_f}F_v^\times\right).$$
  - Explanation: A smooth vector can be chosen whose finite Whittaker function is the indicator of a small compact open unit subgroup.
- **claim-sheet-profile** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-sheet-profile)): Let $\mathcal I$ be a finite index set. For every $\kappa\in\mathcal I$, let $f_\kappa$ be a finite sum of products $$f_\kappa(y)=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L_\kappa} \prod_{v\in S}f_{\kappa,\ell,v}(y_v), \qquad y\in F_S^\times.$$ Assume that for one $\delta>0$ all these local factors satisfy the following conditions.* 1. *At a finite $v$, $f_{\kappa,\ell,v}$ is locally constant, vanishes for $\|y_v\|_v>C_v$, and is $O(\|y_v\|_v^\delta)$ for $\|y_v\|_v\le1$.* 2. *At a complex $v$, the factor is smooth on $F_v^\times$ and, for every $j\ge0$ and $A>0$, $$\|\mathcal D_v^j f_{\kappa,\ell,v}(y_v)\| \le C_{A,j}\min(\|y_v\|_v^\delta,\|y_v\|_v^{-A}).$$* *All constants may depend on the finite family. Define $$H_\kappa(u)=\int_{X_u}f_\kappa(y)\,\,\mathrm{d}^\times_u y,\qquad D=u\frac{\,\mathrm{d}}{\,\mathrm{d}u}.$$ Fix one complex place $v_0$, and define the nonnegative absolute derivative profile $$H^{\mathrm{abs}}_{\kappa,j}(u) =\sum_{\ell=1}^{L_\kappa}\int_{X_u} \|\mathcal D_{v_0}^j f_{\kappa,\ell,v_0}(y_{v_0})\| \prod_{v\ne v_0}\|f_{\kappa,\ell,v}(y_v)\|\,\,\mathrm{d}^\times_u y.$$ Then $H_\kappa$ is smooth, and for every $j\ge0$ and $\sigma>-\delta$, $$\label{eq:sheet-weighted-bound} \|D^jH_\kappa(u)\|\le H^{\mathrm{abs}}_{\kappa,j}(u) \le C_{\sigma,j}u^{-\sigma}\qquad(u>0).$$ In particular, for any $0<\alpha<\delta$ and every $A>0$, $$\label{eq:sheet-two-ended-bound} \|D^jH_\kappa(u)\|+H^{\mathrm{abs}}_{\kappa,j}(u) \le C_{A,j}\min(u^\alpha,u^{-A}).$$ The conclusions are unchanged if each $f_\kappa$ is multiplied by a fixed smooth unitary character of $F_S^\times$ and by a bounded function of its class in the finite group $\mathcal V$.* *Let $\mathcal K:(0,\infty)\to\mathbb{C}$ have every absolute moment $$M_\beta(\mathcal K)=\int_0^\infty\|\mathcal K(T)\|T^\beta \,\frac{\,\mathrm{d}T}{T}<\infty \qquad(\beta\in\mathbb{R}).$$ Both convolutions $$\int_0^\infty\mathcal K(T)H_\kappa(uT)\,\frac{\,\mathrm{d}T}{T}, \qquad \Phi_\kappa(u):=\int_0^\infty\mathcal K(T)H_\kappa(u/T) \,\frac{\,\mathrm{d}T}{T}$$ and their norm derivatives obey <a href="#eq:sheet-two-ended-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:sheet-two-ended-bound">[eq:sheet-two-ended-bound]</a>, with constants depending also on the indicated moments. For every $j$, the two absolute majorants $\int\|\mathcal K(T)\|H^{\mathrm{abs}}_{\kappa,j}(uT^{\pm1})\,\,\mathrm{d}T/T$ satisfy the same two-ended size bound. For any $x_i>0$, complex $a_i$, indices $\kappa_i\in\mathcal I$, $\tau\in\{1,-1\}$, and $\sigma>-\delta$, one has the absolute bound $$\label{eq:sheet-absolute-sum} \begin{split} &\int_0^\infty\|\mathcal K(T)\| \sum_i\|a_i\|H^{\mathrm{abs}}_{\kappa_i,0}(u x_iT^\tau) \,\frac{\,\mathrm{d}T}{T}\\ &\hspace{12mm}\le C_{\sigma,0}u^{-\sigma}M_{-\tau\sigma}(\mathcal K) \sum_i\|a_i\|x_i^{-\sigma}. \end{split}$$ In particular the sum and integral may be interchanged if the final series is finite.
  - Explanation: Integrals over norm sheets of finite sums of local products are smooth, with two-ended decay bounds for derivatives and for convolutions against rapidly decaying kernels.
- **claim-gaussian-smoothing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-gaussian-smoothing)): For $V$ in <a href="#eq:theta-V-profile" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:theta-V-profile">[eq:theta-V-profile]</a>, there are constants $C_0>0$ and $A_0>0$ such that $$\label{eq:theta-V-mellin} \widehat V(s) =C_0 A_0^s \bigl[\Gamma(s+5/6)\Gamma(s+7/6)\bigr]^r \qquad(\operatorname{Re}s>-5/6).$$ There is a smooth function $\mathcal K:(0,\infty)\to\mathbb{C}$ such that, for every $N>0$ and $j\ge0$, $$\label{eq:theta-kernel-bounds} \left\|(T\partial_T)^j\mathcal K(T)\right\| \le C_{N,j}\min(T^N,T^{-N}),$$ and, for every $u>0$, $$\label{eq:theta-exact-gaussian} \int_0^\infty\mathcal K(T)V(uT)\,\frac{\,\mathrm{d}T}{T} =P_G(u)=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\pi}} \exp\!\left(-\frac{(\log u)^2}{4}\right).$$ Its Mellin transform satisfies the exact sign convention $$\label{eq:theta-kernel-mellin} \widehat{\mathcal K}(-s) =\frac{e^{s^2}}{\widehat V(s)}.$$ The quotient on the right is interpreted by its entire continuation. For every fixed finite family of rotated $S$-profiles $H_\kappa$ covered by [lem:sheet-profile](#lem:sheet-profile), the functions $\Phi_\kappa(u)=\int\mathcal K(T)H_\kappa(u/T)\,\,\mathrm{d}T/T$ obey the signed and absolute bounds of that lemma.
  - Explanation: A rapidly decaying smooth kernel exists whose multiplicative convolution with the radial profile is exactly the Gaussian weight, via an explicit Mellin identity.
- **claim-reflection-theta-representation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-reflection-theta-representation)): Let $V$ and $\mathcal K$ be the common unrotated profile and Gaussian averaging weight from [lem:gaussian-smoothing](#lem:gaussian-smoothing). For $l,Z>0$, $$\begin{aligned} \mathcal T_m(l) &=\sum_{\substack{\mathfrak c\ \mathrm{squarefree}\\\mathfrak n}} \frac{\gamma_2(\mathfrak c)}{q_{\mathfrak c}^{1/2}q_{\mathfrak n}} L(A)\prod_{\mathfrak p\mid\mathfrak m} \chi_{\mathfrak p}(A)^{j_{\mathfrak p}} V(q_{\mathfrak A}l), \label{eq:reflection-unreflected}\\ B_m(Z) &=\int_0^\infty\mathcal K(T)\mathcal T_m(T/Z)\,\frac{\,\mathrm{d}T}{T}. \label{eq:reflection-exact-average} \end{aligned}$$ Here $\mathfrak A=\mathfrak c\mathfrak n^3$ and $A=cn^3$ is an allowed product generator. The ideal sums are over integral exterior ideals. Both formulas converge absolutely after the sheet integrations, and the second converges absolutely after the $T$-integration.
  - Explanation: The smoothed coefficient sum is represented as a Gaussian-weighted average of the quotient theta sum, with absolutely convergent exchanges.
- **claim-local-weyl** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-reflection-local-weyl)): Let $\mathfrak p$ be an exterior prime and put $W_e=W_{\mathfrak p}(a(p^e))$. For $j\in\{0,\ldots,5\}$, a unit $t$, and an integer $e$, define $$\mathcal R_{\mathfrak p,j,e}(t) =\sum_{v\in k_{\mathfrak p}^{\times}}\alpha_{\mathfrak p,j}(v) W_{\mathfrak p}(a(p^{e-2}t)w n(v/p)).$$ It vanishes for $e<0$. For $e\ge0$, $$\label{eq:reflection-local-formula} \mathcal R_{\mathfrak p,j,e}(t) =W_e\chi_{\mathfrak p}(t)^{4+2e} \sum_{v\in k_{\mathfrak p}^{\times}} \alpha_{\mathfrak p,j}(v)\chi_{\mathfrak p}(v)^4 \psi_{\mathfrak p}(p^{e-1}t/v).$$ For $j=1$, it vanishes for $e>0$, while $$\label{eq:reflection-valuation-one} \begin{split} \mathcal R_{\mathfrak p,1,0}(t) &=\omega_{\mathfrak p}\chi_{\mathfrak p}(t), \qquad \|\omega_{\mathfrak p}\|=1,\\ \omega_{\mathfrak p} &=\frac{\sum_{x\ne0}\chi_{\mathfrak p}(x)^3\psi_{\mathfrak p}(x/p)} {\tau_{\mathfrak p,1}}. \end{split}$$ For all $j$, its magnitude at $e=0$ is at most $1$, and for $e>0$ is at most $q_{\mathfrak p}^{1/2}\|W_e\|$. More precisely, for $e\ge0$ put $s_{\mathfrak p,j,e}=\mathcal R_{\mathfrak p,j,e}(1)$ and choose the exponents modulo $6$ by $$\label{eq:reflection-local-monomial} b_{j,0}\equiv8-j,\qquad b_{j,e}\equiv4+2e\ (e>0),\qquad \mathcal R_{\mathfrak p,j,e}(t) =s_{\mathfrak p,j,e}\chi_{\mathfrak p}(t)^{b_{j,e}}.$$ For $e>0$, $s_{\mathfrak p,j,e}=0$ unless $j=4$, and it also vanishes for $e\equiv2\pmod3$. The zero-digit term is available only for $j=0$; at a frequency $\nu$ it is $(1-q_{\mathfrak p}^{-1})W_{\mathfrak p}(a(\nu))$, with ordinary spherical support.
  - Explanation: At an exterior prime the reflected local factor vanishes in negative valuation, is an explicit unit-modulus phase in the valuation-one case, and is bounded otherwise.
- **claim-reflected-expansion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-reflection-quadratic-pairing)): For every nonzero $m\in R$ and $Z>0$, let $(m)_S=\mathfrak h\mathfrak d$ and $\upsilon=\upsilon_m$. Then $$\label{eq:reflection-global-expansion} \begin{split} B_m(Z) ={}&\sum_{\mathfrak f\in\mathcal F(\mathfrak h)} \sum_{\substack{\mathfrak a'_{\mathfrak f}\in\mathcal A(\mathfrak f)\\ \mathfrak n'\\(\mathfrak n',\mathfrak f)=1}} \sum_{\substack{u\in U_6\\s_d,s_c\in B}} \frac{\omega_{\upsilon,\mathfrak t}}{q_{\mathfrak n'}}\\ &{}\times\rho_{\upsilon,\mathfrak t,s_d}(\mathfrak d) \Biggl[\sum_{\mathfrak c'\ \mathrm{squarefree}} \frac{\lambda_{\upsilon,\mathfrak t,s_c}(\mathfrak c')} {q_{\mathfrak c'}^{1/2}} \chi_{\mathfrak d}(c')^3\\ &{}\times\Phi_{\upsilon,\kappa_6(\mathfrak t;s_d,s_c)} \!\left(\frac{Zq_{\mathfrak a'_{\mathfrak f}} q_{\mathfrak c'}q_{\mathfrak n'}^3} {q_{\mathfrak d}^2q_{\mathfrak f}^2}\right)\Biggr]. \end{split}$$ All ideal sums are over integral exterior ideals, and the expansion is absolutely convergent. The coefficients satisfy $$\|\omega_{\upsilon,\mathfrak t}\| \le\beta_{\mathfrak f}(\mathfrak a'_{\mathfrak f}),\qquad \|\rho_{\upsilon,\mathfrak t,s_d}\|\le1,\qquad \|\lambda_{\upsilon,\mathfrak t,s_c}\|\le1.$$ The row-unit class $\upsilon$ is fixed; $u$ is the independently summed frequency-unit class.
  - Explanation: The reflected expansion separates row, column and frozen factors, coupled only through a quadratic symbol and a smooth norm profile.
- **claim-quadratic-sieve** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-reflection-quadratic-sieve)): For $C,D\geq1$ and complex coefficients $z_{\mathfrak c}$ supported on squarefree exterior ideals with $q_{\mathfrak c}\leq C$, one has, for every $\epsilon>0$, $$\label{eq:reflection-quadratic-sieve} \sum_{\substack{\mathfrak d\ \mathrm{squarefree}\\q_{\mathfrak d}\leq D}} \left\|\sum_{\substack{\mathfrak c\ \mathrm{squarefree}\\q_{\mathfrak c}\leq C}} z_{\mathfrak c}\chi_{\mathfrak d}(c)^3\right\|^2 \ll_{F,S,\epsilon}(C+D)(CD)^\epsilon \sum_{\mathfrak c}\|z_{\mathfrak c}\|^2.$$ Arbitrary restrictions on the rows and separate restrictions or unitary factors on the columns are allowed by deleting rows and changing the coefficients.
  - Explanation: A quadratic large sieve over the number field bounds the mean square of bilinear sums of the quadratic symbol with a loss only in the sum of the lengths.
- **claim-additive-sieve** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-additive-sieve)): Let $K$ be a fixed compact subset of $F_S$, and let $N,V\ge2$. Choose balanced generators $s$ for the exterior ideals with $q_{\mathfrak s}\le V$. Let $\mathcal P$ be any subset of the distinct points $$\left\{\frac d s+R: q_{\mathfrak s}\le V,\ d\in(R/\mathfrak s)^\times\right\} \subset F_S/R.$$ For any complex $b_\alpha$, $$\sum_{m\in R\cap\ell_NK} \left\|\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal P}b_\alpha\mathrm{e}(m\alpha)\right\|^2 \ll_{F,S,K}(N+V^2)\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal P}\|b_\alpha\|^2 . \label{eq:additive-sieve}$$ The dual inequality, with the sums over $m$ and $\alpha$ interchanged, has the same constant.
  - Explanation: Well-separated additive fractions with bounded denominator norm satisfy a large sieve inequality over S-integers in a compact box.
- **claim-probe-low** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-probe-low)): For the weights above, for every $\epsilon>0$ and $Z\ge2$, $$I(Z^a,Z^b,Z)\ll_{F,S,\eta,W_0,W_1,\epsilon} Z^{(2M_0-1-b)/2+\epsilon} =Z^{933/2000+\epsilon}. \label{eq:probe-low}$$
  - Explanation: Combining the reflected moment with the additive sieve bounds the additive probe by a fixed power of Z.
- **claim-euler-product** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-euler-product)): For every $u\in\mathcal U$, the series in [eq:calF-definition](#eq:calF-definition) has the Euler factorization $$\mathcal F_u(\xi,w,z)= \frac{\zeta_F^S(6z)L^S(w,\chi_\bullet(u))} {L^S(\xi,\eta\overline{\chi_\bullet(u)})} \mathcal H_u(\xi,w,z) \label{eq:probe-euler-product}$$ initially in absolute convergence. The correction $\mathcal H_u$ is holomorphic in open neighborhoods of both real-part regions $$\begin{array}{lll} \operatorname{Re}\xi\ge .998,&\operatorname{Re}w\ge .003,&\operatorname{Re}z\ge .4,\\ \operatorname{Re}\xi\ge .998,&\operatorname{Re}w\ge .95,&\operatorname{Re}z\ge .16. \end{array} \label{eq:euler-regions}$$ For every $\epsilon>0$ it satisfies $$\|\mathcal H_u(\xi,w,z)\|\ll_{F,S,\eta,\epsilon}q_u^\epsilon$$ there, uniformly in all heights. If $S$ contains every prime of norm at most a sufficiently large fixed constant, then $\mathcal H_1$ is bounded and nowhere zero on both neighborhoods.
  - Explanation: The probe Dirichlet series factors as a zeta factor times a numerator Hecke function over the reciprocal target function times a holomorphic correction, nonvanishing for the principal row.
- **claim-conductor-sieve** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-conductor-sieve)): Fix $C_0>0$ and a finite set $\mathscr L_S$ of possible local components at $S$. Let $\mathscr X(U)$ be any family of distinct primitive finite-order Hecke characters of $F$ whose conductor norms are at most $C_0U$ and whose components at $S$ belong to that set. For $N,U\ge2$, any complex coefficients $b_{\mathfrak n}$, and every $\epsilon>0$, $$\sum_{\omega\in\mathscr X(U)} \left\|\sum_{q_{\mathfrak n}\le N} b_{\mathfrak n}\omega(\mathfrak n)\right\|^2 \ll_{F,S,C_0,\mathscr L_S,\epsilon} (N+U^2)(NU)^\epsilon \sum_{q_{\mathfrak n}\le N}\|b_{\mathfrak n}\|^2 . \label{eq:conductor-sieve}$$ As usual a primitive character is extended by zero on ideals meeting its conductor.
  - Explanation: A large sieve inequality holds for families of distinct primitive finite-order Hecke characters of bounded conductor norm.
- **claim-zero-density** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-zero-density)): For the fixed $F,S,\eta$ there are $\rho>0$ and $U_0$ with the following property. For every $U\ge U_0$, there is a set $\mathcal E(U)\subset\{u\in\mathcal U:U\le q_u<2U\}$ of cardinality $$\|\mathcal E(U)\|\ll_{F,S,\eta}U^{1/2}$$ which contains every row for which $\omega_u$ is principal or $L_F(s,\omega_u)$ has a zero with $\operatorname{Re}s\ge .996$ and $\|\operatorname{Im}s\|\le4U^\rho$. For $u\notin\mathcal E(U)$ and every $\epsilon>0$, $$\left\|L^S(\xi,\eta\overline{\chi_\bullet(u)})^{-1}\right\| \ll_{F,S,\eta,\epsilon}U^\epsilon \qquad (\operatorname{Re}\xi\ge .998,\ \|\operatorname{Im}\xi\|\le2U^\rho). \label{eq:good-reciprocal}$$ The exponents $.996,.998,1/2$ are absolute. The height exponent $\rho$, the constants, and $U_0$ may depend on the fixed data.
  - Explanation: Outside a small exceptional set of frequency rows the reciprocal Hecke function is bounded by a small power of the row size on a rectangle near the line of absolute convergence.
- **claim-field-bounds** (theorem, [source](#stmt-spl-field-bounds)): Fix an integer $a$ with $\|a\|>1$. For every sufficiently large prime $q$, the degree $n_q=[K_q:\mathbb{Q}]$ and absolute discriminant $D_q$ satisfy $$n_q=q(q-1),\qquad D_q\le q^{q(q-2)}(q^q\|a\|^{q-1})^{q-1},\qquad \log D_q+n_q\ll_a n_q\log(2q). \label{spl:degree-discriminant}$$ Every rational prime $p\nmid aq$ is unramified in $K_q$, and $$\zeta_{K_q}(s)\ne0 \qquad(\operatorname{Re}s>1-\delta_0,\ s\ne1). \label{spl:kummer-zero-free}$$
  - Explanation: The Kummer field has explicit degree, a discriminant bound, controlled ramification, and its Dedekind zeta function inherits the common zero-free width.
- **claim-smooth-estimate** (bound, [source](#stmt-spl-smooth-estimate)): Fix $0<\delta<1/2$ and a nonnegative function $f\in C_c^\infty((0,\infty))$. Let $K$ be a number field of degree $n$ and absolute discriminant $D$ whose Dedekind zeta function has no zero in $\operatorname{Re}s>1-\delta$. For every real $x\ge2$, $$\Theta_{K,f}(x) :=\sum_{\mathfrak p}\sum_{j\ge1} (\log\operatorname{N}\mathfrak p) f\left(\frac{(\operatorname{N}\mathfrak p)^j}{x}\right) \ll_f x+x^{1-\delta}(\log D+n), \label{spl:smooth-prime-ideal-bound}$$ where $\mathfrak p$ ranges over the nonzero prime ideals of $K$. The implied constant is independent of $K$ and $x$.
  - Explanation: A smoothed sum over prime ideal powers is bounded by the main term plus a saving whose size is linear in the log discriminant and degree.
- **claim-marked-type-ii** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-marked-type-ii)): Fix $\delta,C,D_*>0$ and $q\in(0,1)$. Suppose $H_m,H_n\ge x^\delta$ and $X=H_mH_n\asymp x$. Let $F$ satisfy $\|F(h)\|\le1$ and $F(ph)=F(h)$ for every prime in the groups defined in [eq:marked-prime-groups](#eq:marked-prime-groups). Let $\alpha_m$ be supported on an arbitrary interval in $[H_m,2H_m]$, where it has the value $$\alpha_m=m^{iv}\left(\mathbf{1}_{m\text{ prime}} -\frac{\mathbf{1}_{P^-(m)>W}}{V(W)\log m}\right), \qquad \|v\|\le L^C.$$ Let $\beta_n$ be supported on $W$-rough integers in $[H_n,2H_n]$, with $\|\beta_n\|\le L^C$. If $K$ is sufficiently large in terms of $\delta,C,D_*,q$, then $$\label{eq:marked-type-ii} \left\|\sum_{m,n}\alpha_m\beta_n F(mn-1)\mathcal W(mn-1)\right\| \ll X L^{-D_*}.$$ The required lower bound on $K$ is independent of the particular $a_i$. The implicit constant and the threshold for $x$ may depend on all the fixed parameters, including the $a_i$.
  - Explanation: Imported one-sided marked bilinear estimate showing arbitrary logarithmic cancellation between a prime-indicator coefficient and its rough comparison.
- **claim-coefficient-transfer** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-marked-coefficient-transfer)): Fix $\delta,C,D_*>0$ and $q\in(0,1)$. Retain the marked data and the hypotheses on $F,H_m,H_n$ in [thm:marked-type-ii](#thm:marked-type-ii), with fixed comparison constants in $X=H_mH_n\asymp x$. Let $(\alpha_m)$ and $(\beta_n)$ be scalar sequences indexed by positive integers, supported on $W$-rough integers in their respective dyads, with $\alpha$ supported on an arbitrary interval $I\subseteq[H_m,2H_m]$, and with $\|\alpha_m\|,\|\beta_n\|\le L^C$. Suppose that for every fixed $A_0,B,A>0$, every Dirichlet character $\chi$ modulo $k\le L^{A_0}$, and every $\|t\|\le2XL^B$, $$\label{eq:type-ii-long-coefficient-interface} \left\|\frac1{H_m}\sum_m\alpha_m\chi(m)m^{it}\right\|\ll_A L^{-A},$$ uniformly in the permitted interval, character, and frequency. The constant and threshold in this hypothesis may depend on $A_0,B,A$, the fixed setup, and all further fixed parameters defining the coefficients; these parameters are chosen before $x$ grows.* *Then [eq:marked-type-ii](#eq:marked-type-ii) holds when $K$ is sufficiently large in terms of the fixed data, independently of the particular band exponents $a_i$. Its implicit constant and threshold may depend on all the fixed data, including the $a_i$.
  - Explanation: The marked estimate holds for any scalar coefficients that cancel against every character of logarithmic modulus over the stated frequency range.
- **claim-rough-type-ii** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-rough-type-ii)): Retain the marked data and all the hypotheses on $F,\beta,H_m,H_n$ of [thm:marked-type-ii](#thm:marked-type-ii). Fix real numbers $0<\gamma<w_-<w_+<1$, and suppose $$[\log H_m/L,\log(2H_m)/L]\subseteq[w_-,w_+].$$ On an arbitrary interval $I\subseteq[H_m,2H_m]$, set $$\label{eq:rough-type-ii-coefficient} \alpha_m=m^{iv}\left( \mathbf{1}_{P^-(m)>x^\gamma} -\frac{D_\gamma(\log m/L)}{L V(W)}\mathbf{1}_{P^-(m)>W} \right),\qquad \|v\|\le L^C,$$ and set $\alpha_m=0$ outside $I$. Then [eq:marked-type-ii](#eq:marked-type-ii) holds for every fixed $D_*>0$ when $K$ is sufficiently large in terms of the fixed data. Its required lower bound is independent of the particular band exponents $a_i$; the threshold and implicit constant may depend on all the fixed data. In particular, $\gamma$ and the gap $w_--\gamma$ are fixed before $x$ grows.
  - Explanation: The criterion is verified for the rough-factor discrepancy against the continuous density, giving the marked bilinear bound.
- **claim-block-sieve** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-block)): Let $\mathcal A$ be a finite set with nonnegative weights, and let $\mathcal P$ be a set of primes at most $z$, where $z\ge2$. For each $p\in\mathcal P$, specify a bad condition on $\mathcal A$. Suppose that, for every squarefree product $d$ of primes in $\mathcal P$, the weight of the objects satisfying all conditions at $p\mid d$ is $$A(d)=X'g(d)+E(d),$$ including $d=1$, where $X'\ge0$, $g(1)=1$, and $g$ is multiplicative. Assume that, for fixed constants $\eta_0>0$ and $C_0<\infty$, $$\label{eq:block-hypotheses} 0\le g(p)\le1-\eta_0, \qquad \sum_{\substack{v<p\le v^2\\p\in\mathcal P}}g(p)\le C_0 \quad(v>1).$$ For every sufficiently large even integer $H$, the weight $S$ of objects avoiding all the bad conditions satisfies $$\label{eq:block-sieve} S=X'\prod_{p\in\mathcal P}(1-g(p)) \bigl(1+O(\exp(-H))\bigr) +O\left(\sum_{\substack{d\le z^{4H+2}\\d\mid\prod_{p\in\mathcal P}p}} \|E(d)\|\right).$$ The constants and the lower bound on $H$ depend only on $\eta_0,C_0$. In particular, the estimate is uniform when $H$ grows. For $H=2$ there is the upper bound $$\label{eq:block-sieve-upper} S\ll_{\eta_0,C_0}X'\prod_{p\in\mathcal P}(1-g(p)) +\sum_{\substack{d\le z^{10}\\d\mid\prod_{p\in\mathcal P}p}} \|E(d)\|.$$
  - Explanation: A Brun-Hooley block sieve gives the sifted mass up to a small relative error plus remainders with coefficients bounded by one.
- **claim-rough-density** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-density)): For $0<b<1/2$, $$\label{eq:density-identity} \int_b^{1/2}D_t(1-t)\,\frac{dt}{t}=D_b(1)-1,$$ where the integrand at $1/2$ is interpreted by its left limit. There are absolute constants $b_0,c_d,C_d>0$ such that, for $0<b<b_0$, $$\label{eq:density-upper} D_b(1)\le\frac{\exp(-\gamma_E)}{b} \bigl(1+C_d\exp(-c_d/b)\bigr).$$ One may take $c_d=1/20$.
  - Explanation: A least-prime-factor identity for the rough-number density and an upper bound with exponentially small correction.
- **claim-progression-type-ii** (bound, [source](#stmt-con-type-ii)): Fix $\delta>0$ and $0<\gamma<s_-<s_+<1$. Let $H_m,H_n\ge x^\delta$, with $X=H_mH_n\asymp x$ and fixed comparison constants, and let $I\subseteq[H_m,2H_m]$ be an interval. Suppose $$\label{con:exponent-range} [\log H_m/L,\log(2H_m)/L]\subseteq[s_-,s_+].$$ Let $\|b_n\|\le1$ be supported on $W$-rough integers in $[H_n,2H_n]$. For sufficiently large $K$, $$\label{con:type-ii-bound} \left\|\sum_{m\in I,n} \bigl(R_\gamma(m)-B_\gamma(m)\bigr)b_n w(mn)\right\| \ll XL^{-6}.$$ This is uniform in the dyads, interval, and coefficients. The lower bound on $K$ is independent of the $a_i$. Constants and thresholds may depend on all fixed parameters, including the band exponents.
  - Explanation: The marked bilinear estimate holds for weighted integers in the fixed progression after expanding the congruence in characters and separating the smooth weight.
- **claim-harmonic-mass** (theorem, [source](#stmt-con-harmonic)): For each group put $$E_i(t)=\prod_{p\in\mathcal P_i}\left(1+\frac{t}{p-1}\right).$$ The harmonic mass has the exact Euler-product expression $$\label{con:harmonic-identity} \begin{aligned} J_0&=\prod_{i=1}^K\frac{E_i'(1/2)}{V_i}\\ &=\prod_{i=1}^K\left\{ \frac{E_i(1/2)}{V_i} \sum_{p\in\mathcal P_i}\frac1{p-1+1/2}\right\}. \end{aligned}$$ For every fixed $\varepsilon>0$, $$\label{con:harmonic-bounds} 1\le J_0\ll_K1,\qquad \sum_{r>x^\varepsilon}\frac{\mathcal W(r)}r \ll_K\exp(-\varepsilon L^{1-a_K}).$$ Consequently the part of $X_0$ with $r>x^\varepsilon$ is $O_K(x\exp(-\varepsilon L^{1-a_K}))$.
  - Explanation: The harmonic mass has an exact Euler-product expression, is bounded above and below, and its tail beyond small powers of x is negligible.
- **claim-distribution** (bound, [source](#stmt-con-distribution)): For every fixed $A>0$, $$\label{con:bv-sum} \sum_{r\le x^\varepsilon}\mathcal W(r) \sum_{\substack{\ell\le D\\\ell\text{ odd squarefree}}}\|E_r(\ell)\| \ll xJ_0L^{-A}.$$ Moreover, writing $A_\Psi=\int_1^2\Psi(y)\,dy$, we have $$\label{con:mass-asymptotic} X_0\sim\frac{xJ_0A_\Psi}{c\varphi(M/c)L} \qquad(x\to\infty),$$ with all construction parameters fixed and $J_0=J_0(x)$. In particular, $$\label{con:total-mass} X_0\asymp_{M,\Psi}\frac{xJ_0}{L},$$ with comparison constants independent of $K,\kappa,\varepsilon,b$.
  - Explanation: Level-of-distribution estimates hold for the weighted family, giving the total mass up to logarithmic factors.
- **claim-bin-mass** (theorem, [source](#stmt-con-bin-mass)): For any fixed $b\le\gamma<\gamma'\le1/2-\kappa$, $$\label{con:bin-mass-formula} \sum_{\substack{x^\gamma<n\le x^{\gamma'}\\n\text{ prime}}} \sum_{\substack{n\mid d\\P^-(d)>W}}w(d) =\mathfrak S_M X_0 V(W) \left(\log\frac{\gamma'}\gamma+o(1)\right).$$
  - Explanation: The mass conditioned on a prime divisor in a logarithmic bin matches the Mertens-type main term.
- **claim-rough-pairs** (bound, [source](#stmt-con-rough-pairs)): Let $\mathcal B=[M_1,2M_1)\times[M_2,2M_2)$ be any dyadic box such that $$\label{con:rough-box-range} x/4\le M_1M_2\le2x,\qquad x^{0.46}/2\le M_i\le2x^{0.54}\quad(i=1,2).$$ Then $$\label{con:rough-pair-bound} \sum_{\substack{(m,n)\in\mathcal B\\P^-(m)>W,\ P^-(n)>W}}w(mn) \le C_1X_0V(W)^2,$$ where $C_1$ depends at most on $M,\Psi$, and is independent of $K,\kappa,b,\varepsilon$.
  - Explanation: Rough pairs in a balanced dyadic box carry mass bounded by an absolute multiple of the total mass times the squared small-prime product.

      "exact_statement": "Let $a\\ne0$ be an integer, and let $p,q$ be primes with $p\\nmid aq$. Then $p$ splits completely in $K_q$ if and only if $$p\\equiv1\\pmod q,\\qquad a^{(p-1)/q}\\equiv1\\pmod p.\n \\label{eq:kummer-frobenius-test}$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-auto-remark-1",
        "exact_quote": "Negative squares are covered by the row $d_0=-1$ in the progression lemma. Odd perfect powers also require no separate exclusion. If $a=b^h$ with fixed odd $h>1$, every prime divisor of $h$ is eventually smaller than every prime factor of $rQ$; hence $(h,p-1)=1$ for the constructed primes. This explains directly how the controlled predecessor removes the obstruction from the fixed exponent.",
        "source_id": "PM-029.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

This explains directly how the controlled predecessor removes the obstruction from the fixed exponent.",
          "source_id": "PM-029.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-s-lattice",
        "exact_quote": "The diagonal copy of $R$ is a discrete cocompact subgroup of $F_S$, is its own annihilator for the pairing $\\mathrm{e}(xy)$, and has covolume $1$. For every Bruhat–Schwartz function $f$ on $F_S$, $$\\sum_{m\\in R}f(m)=\\sum_{\\nu\\in R}\\widehat f(\\nu).$$ If $\\mathfrak a=aR$ is an exterior fractional ideal, then $$q_a=q_{\\mathfrak a},\\qquad\n (aR)^\\perp=a^{-1}R,\\qquad\n \\sum_{m\\in aR}f(m)\n   =q_{\\mathfrak a}^{-1}\\sum_{\\nu\\in a^{-1}R}\\widehat f(\\nu).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-029.1"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-029.1"

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-balanced-generators",
        "exact_quote": "The pairing $H_S$ on $\\mathcal V$ is nondegenerate and alternating. Any two such generators differ by an element of $U_S^6$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-kummer-characters",
        "exact_quote": "For $u\\in R\\setminus\\{0\\}$, the ideal function $$\\chi_\\bullet(u)(\\mathfrak a)=\\chi_{\\mathfrak a}(u)$$ is induced away from $S$ and the prime divisors of $(u)R$ by a finite-order Hecke character. Its values at those prime divisors are set equal to zero. If $\\mathfrak f_u$ is the conductor of the inducing primitive character, then $$\\mathrm{N}\\mathfrak f_u\\le C_{F,S}\\mathrm{N}\\mathop{\\mathrm{rad}}((u)R)\n                       \\le C_{F,S}q_u.\\label{eq:kummer-conductor}$$ The same bound, with a changed constant, holds after twisting by the fixed $\\eta$. The inducing characters associated to $u$ and $v$ agree if and only if $u/v\\in F^{\\times6}$.*\n\n*Every character of the finite group $B$, evaluated on allowed generators, extends to a finite-order Hecke character unramified outside $S$.*\n\n*The following parametrization will index the nonzero Poisson frequencies; its character distinctness and conductor bound will be used in zero detection. Choose a complete set of representatives $\\mathcal U$ modulo $U_S^6$ among the nonzero S-integers whose exterior valuations belong to $\\{0,1,\\ldots,5\\}$, with $1\\in\\mathcal U$. For allowed generators $r$, the map $$(u,\\mathfrak r)\\longmapsto [u r^6]\n \\quad\\text{from }\\mathcal U\\times\\{\\text{exterior ideals}\\}\n \\quad\\text{to }(R\\setminus\\{0\\})/U_S^6\\label{eq:frequency-decomposition}$$ is a bijection. Among $u\\in\\mathcal U$, only $u=1$ induces the principal character, and distinct $u$’s induce distinct characters, also after the fixed twist by $\\eta$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-gauss-characters",
        "exact_quote": "There are a unitary Hecke character $\\Lambda$, unramified outside $S$, and a finite-order Hecke character $G$, unramified outside $S$, such that for every exterior prime $\\mathfrak p$, $$\\gamma_2(\\mathfrak p)^3=-\\Lambda(\\mathfrak p),\\qquad\n G(\\mathfrak p)=\\overline{\\chi_{\\mathfrak p}(4)}\\,\n                     \\gamma_3(\\mathfrak p),\\qquad\n \\gamma_1(\\mathfrak p)\\gamma_2(\\mathfrak p)\n                      =-\\Lambda(\\mathfrak p)G(\\mathfrak p).\n\\label{eq:gauss-corrections}$$ At each complex place the infinity type of $\\Lambda$ is purely angular of exponent $1$ or $-1$, in the ordinary angular coordinate. The values $\\gamma_3(\\mathfrak p)$ are signs and depend multiplicatively on the class of an allowed $p$ in $B$.*\n\n*For coprime squarefree exterior ideals, $$\\gamma_j(\\mathfrak c_1\\mathfrak c_2)\n =\\chi_{\\mathfrak c_1}(c_2)^j\n  \\chi_{\\mathfrak c_2}(c_1)^j\n  \\gamma_j(\\mathfrak c_1)\\gamma_j(\\mathfrak c_2).\n\\label{eq:gauss-crt}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-029.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-gauss-characters",
          "exact_quote": "There are a unitary Hecke character $\\Lambda$, unramified outside $S$, and a finite-order Hecke character $G$, unramified outside $S$, such that for every exterior prime $\\mathfrak p$, $$\\gamma_2(\\mathfrak p)^3=-\\Lambda(\\mathfrak p),\\qquad\n G(\\mathfrak p)=\\overline{\\chi_{\\mathfrak p}(4)}\\,\n                     \\gamma_3(\\mathfrak p),\\qquad\n \\gamma_1(\\mathfrak p)\\gamma_2(\\mathfrak p)\n                      =-\\Lambda(\\mathfrak p)G(\\mathfrak p).\n\\label{eq:gauss-corrections}$$ At each complex place the infinity type of $\\Lambda$ is purely angular of exponent $1$ or $-1$, in the ordinary angular coordinate. The values $\\gamma_3(\\mathfrak p)$ are signs and depend multiplicatively on the class of an allowed $p$ in $B$.*\n\n*For coprime squarefree exterior ideals, $$\\gamma_j(\\mathfrak c_1\\mathfrak c_2)\n =\\chi_{\\mathfrak c_1}(c_2)^j\n  \\chi_{\\mathfrak c_2}(c_1)^j\n  \\gamma_j(\\mathfrak c_1)\\gamma_j(\\mathfrak c_2).\n\\label{eq:gauss-crt}$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-reflection",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F$ be a fixed cyclotomic field containing $\\mu_{12}$, let $S$ and the arithmetic data be as above, and fix a compact subset $\\mathscr K\\subset F_S^\\times$. For every $\\epsilon>0$, every $Z\\geq2$, and all dyadic lengths $P,D\\geq1$, $$\\label{eq:reflection-dyadic-mass}\n \\sum_{\\substack{m\\in\\mathscr B_Z(\\mathscr K)\\\\\n        P\\leq q_{\\mathfrak h(m)}<2P\\\\\n        D\\leq q_{\\mathfrak d(m)}<2D}}\n |B_m(Z)|^2\n \\ll_{F,S,\\eta,\\mathscr K,\\epsilon}\n Z^\\epsilon P^{1/2}\\left(D+\\frac{D^2P}{Z}\\right).$$ In particular, $$\\label{eq:reflection-total-mass}\n \\sum_{m\\in\\mathscr B_Z(\\mathscr K)}|B_m(Z)|^2\n \\ll_{F,S,\\eta,\\mathscr K,\\epsilon} Z^{2M_0-1+\\epsilon}.$$ The estimate is uniform if $\\mathscr K$ is multiplied by an element of any fixed compact subset of the norm-one group of $F_S^\\times$.",
        "source_id": "PM-029.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-reflection",
          "exact_quote": "Let $F$ be a fixed cyclotomic field containing $\\mu_{12}$, let $S$ and the arithmetic data be as above, and fix a compact subset $\\mathscr K\\subset F_S^\\times$. For every $\\epsilon>0$, every $Z\\geq2$, and all dyadic lengths $P,D\\geq1$, $$\\label{eq:reflection-dyadic-mass}\n \\sum_{\\substack{m\\in\\mathscr B_Z(\\mathscr K)\\\\\n        P\\leq q_{\\mathfrak h(m)}<2P\\\\\n        D\\leq q_{\\mathfrak d(m)}<2D}}\n |B_m(Z)|^2\n \\ll_{F,S,\\eta,\\mathscr K,\\epsilon}\n Z^\\epsilon P^{1/2}\\left(D+\\frac{D^2P}{Z}\\right).$$ In particular, $$\\label{eq:reflection-total-mass}\n \\sum_{m\\in\\mathscr B_Z(\\mathscr K)}|B_m(Z)|^2\n \\ll_{F,S,\\eta,\\mathscr K,\\epsilon} Z^{2M_0-1+\\epsilon}.$$ The estimate is uniform if $\\mathscr K$ is multiplied by an element of any fixed compact subset of the norm-one group of $F_S^\\times$.",
          "source_id": "PM-029.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $F$ be a fixed cyclotomic field containing $\\mu_{12}$, let $S$ and the arithmetic data be as above, and fix a compact subset $\\mathscr K\\subset F_S^\\times$. For every $\\epsilon>0$, every $Z\\geq2$, and all dyadic lengths $P,D\\geq1$, $$\\label{eq:reflection-dyadic-mass}\n \\sum_{\\substack{m\\in\\mathscr B_Z(\\mathscr K)\\\\\n        P\\leq q_{\\mathfrak h(m)}<2P\\\\\n        D\\leq q_{\\mathfrak d(m)}<2D}}\n |B_m(Z)|^2\n \\ll_{F,S,\\eta,\\mathscr K,\\epsilon}\n Z^\\epsilon P^{1/2}\\left(D+\\frac{D^2P}{Z}\\right).$$ In particular, $$\\label{eq:reflection-total-mass}\n \\sum_{m\\in\\mathscr B_Z(\\mathscr K)}|B_m(Z)|^2\n \\ll_{F,S,\\eta,\\mathscr K,\\epsilon} Z^{2M_0-1+\\epsilon}.$$ The estimate is uniform if $\\mathscr K$ is multiplied by an element of any fixed compact subset of the norm-one group of $F_S^\\times$.",
      "explanation": "The coefficient family has a second moment over balanced annular rows bounded by a power of Z, with a finer dyadic version in the powerful and squarefree parts.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-theta-input",
        "exact_quote": "For the fixed field $F$, there is a threefold central cover $$1\\longrightarrow\\mu_3\n \\longrightarrow\\widetilde{\\mathrm{PGL}}_2(\\mathbb{A}_F)\n \\longrightarrow\\mathrm{PGL}_2(\\mathbb{A}_F)\\longrightarrow1$$ which splits over $\\mathrm{PGL}_2(F)$, and an irreducible genuine automorphic representation $\\Theta=\\bigotimes'_v\\Theta_v$ on this cover, with the following properties.*\n\n\n1.  **Global representation and Fourier expansion.* The cover is the quotient of the $c=2$ cover $\\widetilde{\\mathrm{GL}}_2^{(2)}$ by the ordinary split scalar subgroup. The inducing character of $\\Theta_v$, on the center of the covering torus before this quotient, is $$\\label{eq:theta-central-datum}\n      \\omega_v\\!\\left(\\zeta\\,s_v(\\operatorname{diag}(x^3,y^3))s_v(zI)\\right)\n       =\\iota(\\zeta)\\left|x^3/y^3\\right|_v^{1/6}.$$ Here $s_v$ is the ordinary torus section. The normalized inducing norm exponents are $1/6,-1/6$. The local exceptional representation is the irreducible quotient for these exponents and the irreducible subrepresentation for their Weyl conjugates. Every $\\Theta_v$ has a one-dimensional Whittaker space. The global functional $$\\lambda(\\varphi)=\n        \\int_{F\\backslash\\mathbb{A}_F}\\theta_\\varphi(n(x))\n              \\overline{\\psi(x)}\\,\\,\\mathrm{d}x$$ on the automorphic realization $\\varphi\\mapsto\\theta_\\varphi$ is nonzero. The representation is spherical at all but finitely many finite places. There is a preliminary pure tensor $\\varphi^\\circ$ on which $\\lambda$ is nonzero. Its finitely many nondistinguished finite places lie in the final fixed $S$, along with the archimedean places, the places over $6$, and the places where $\\psi_v$ has nonzero conductor.*\n\n\n\n    *For a pure tensor $\\varphi=\\varphi_S\\otimes\\bigotimes_{v\\notin S}\\varphi_v$, with the distinguished spherical vector at almost every $v\\notin S$, the global Whittaker function factors as $$\\label{eq:theta-normalized-factorization}\n      \\mathcal W_\\varphi(g)\n       :=\\lambda(\\pi(g)\\varphi)\n       =\\mathcal W_{S,\\varphi_S}(g_S)\n           \\prod_{v\\notin S}W_{v,\\varphi_v}(g_v).$$ Each exterior spherical functional is normalized at one, so only finitely many factors in this product differ from one for adelic $g$. The functional at $S$ factors at its complex places; at its finite places it may be retained as a finite sum of products of local Whittaker functionals. For a smooth automorphic vector, $$\\label{eq:theta-fourier-expansion}\n       \\theta_\\varphi(g)=\\theta_{\\varphi,N}(g)\n        +\\sum_{\\nu\\in F^\\times}\\mathcal W_\\varphi(a(\\nu)g),\\qquad\n       \\theta_{\\varphi,N}(g)\n        =\\int_{F\\backslash\\mathbb{A}_F}\\theta_\\varphi(n(x)g)\\,\\,\\mathrm{d}x .$$ This expansion converges absolutely and locally uniformly on compact sets of $g$.*\n\n\n2.  **Exact lifts and exterior local values.* On the quotient the chosen lifts satisfy $$\\label{eq:theta-lift-identities}\n      \\begin{gathered}\n       n(x)a(y)=a(y)n(x/y),\\qquad\n       wa(y)w^{-1}=a(y^{-1}),\\\\\n       wn(H)=n(H^{-1})a(-H^{-2})\\bar n(H^{-1}).\n      \\end{gathered}$$ In [(eq:theta-normalized-factorization)](#eq:theta-normalized-factorization) the last identity $H\\ne0$. At an exterior prime, when $H^{-1}$ is integral the last factor is compact, and a compact diagonal may absorb the minus sign.*\n\n\n\n    *For every exterior prime $\\mathfrak p$, choose a uniformizer $\\varpi$, put $q_{\\mathfrak p}=\\mathrm{N}\\mathfrak p$, and use the compact splitting on $\\mathrm{PGL}_2(\\mathcal O_{\\mathfrak p})$. The orientation of $\\iota$, equivalently that of the Hilbert-symbol convention, can be fixed so that the scalar by which the torus cocycle acts in a genuine representation is $$\\label{eq:theta-effective-cocycle}\n       \\mathcal C_{\\mathfrak p}(\\varpi,u)=\\chi_{\\mathfrak p}(u)^{-2}\n           \\qquad(u\\in\\mathcal O_{\\mathfrak p}^{\\times}).$$ The compact splitting agrees with the chosen sections on unit diagonals, Weyl elements, and integral upper and lower root elements. Define $$g_{2,\\mathfrak p}\n        =q_{\\mathfrak p}^{-1/2}\n           \\sum_{x\\bmod\\mathfrak p}\n           \\chi_{\\mathfrak p}(x)^2\\psi_{\\mathfrak p}(x/\\varpi).$$ Then $|g_{2,\\mathfrak p}|=1$. If $W_{\\mathfrak p}$ is the spherical Whittaker function normalized by $W_{\\mathfrak p}(1)=1$, then, for $u\\in\\mathcal O_{\\mathfrak p}^{\\times}$, $$\\label{eq:theta-local-values}\n      W_{\\mathfrak p}(a(\\varpi^e u))\n       =\\chi_{\\mathfrak p}(u)^{2e}\n       \\begin{cases}\n        0,&e<0,\\\\\n        q_{\\mathfrak p}^{-k},&e=3k,\\quad k\\ge0,\\\\\n        q_{\\mathfrak p}^{-k-1/2}g_{2,\\mathfrak p},\n            &e=3k+1,\\quad k\\ge0,\\\\\n        0,&e=3k+2,\\quad k\\ge0.\n       \\end{cases}$$ In particular a shift by three nonnegative valuations multiplies the value by $q_{\\mathfrak p}^{-1}$.*\n\n\n3.  **Complex local behavior.* At a complex place, $\\Theta_v$ is the ordinary spherical $\\mathrm{PGL}_2(\\mathbb{C})$ principal series with normalized characters $(|\\cdot|_v^{1/6},|\\cdot|_v^{-1/6})$, where $|y|_v$ is the square of the ordinary modulus. Its spherical Whittaker function is a nonzero constant times $$\\label{eq:theta-complex-bessel}\n       |y|_v^{1/2}K_{1/3}\\bigl(c_v|y|_v^{1/2}\\bigr),\\qquad c_v>0.$$ The constant term of the automorphic theta function belongs to the Weyl-conjugate torus induction. In particular its dependence on a complex diagonal $a(y)$ is radial, also when the vector on its right has been acted on by a fixed compact element.",
        "source_id": "PM-029.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-theta-exterior-coefficient",
        "exact_quote": "At every exterior prime use an allowed prime generator as the uniformizer in [prop:theta-input](#prop:theta-input). Let $A\\in R\\setminus\\{0\\}$ be an allowed generator of an integral exterior ideal $\\mathfrak A$. Then $$\\label{eq:theta-exterior-coefficient}\n \\prod_{\\mathfrak p\\notin S}W_{\\mathfrak p}(a(A))\n =\n \\begin{cases}\n \\displaystyle\n \\frac{\\gamma_2(\\mathfrak c)}\n      {q_{\\mathfrak c}^{1/2}q_{\\mathfrak n}},\n   &\\mathfrak A=\\mathfrak c\\mathfrak n^3,\\quad\n     \\mathfrak c\\ \\text{squarefree},\\\\[2mm]\n 0,&\\text{otherwise}.\n \\end{cases}$$ In the first case $\\mathfrak c,\\mathfrak n$ are uniquely determined; they need not be coprime.",
        "source_id": "PM-029.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-theta-exterior-coefficient",
          "exact_quote": "At every exterior prime use an allowed prime generator as the uniformizer in [prop:theta-input](#prop:theta-input). Then $$\\label{eq:theta-exterior-coefficient}\n \\prod_{\\mathfrak p\\notin S}W_{\\mathfrak p}(a(A))\n =\n \\begin{cases}\n \\displaystyle\n \\frac{\\gamma_2(\\mathfrak c)}\n      {q_{\\mathfrak c}^{1/2}q_{\\mathfrak n}},\n   &\\mathfrak A=\\mathfrak c\\mathfrak n^3,\\quad\n     \\mathfrak c\\ \\text{squarefree},\\\\[2mm]\n 0,&\\text{otherwise}.\n \\end{cases}$$ In the first case $\\mathfrak c,\\mathfrak n$ are uniquely determined; they need not be coprime.",
          "source_id": "PM-029.1"

      "exact_statement": "At every exterior prime use an allowed prime generator as the uniformizer in [prop:theta-input](#prop:theta-input).     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-whittaker-cutoff",
        "exact_quote": "Let $S_f$ be a finite set of nonarchimedean places and let $\\lambda_f\\ne0$ be a Whittaker functional with character $\\prod_{v\\in S_f}\\psi_v$ on a smooth representation of the product of the local covers. For a vector $\\varphi$, put $$W_\\varphi(y)=\\lambda_f\\!\\left(\\pi\\bigl((a(y_v))_{v\\in S_f}\\bigr)\n                         \\varphi\\right),\n \\qquad y\\in\\prod_{v\\in S_f}F_v^\\times.$$ Given any open subgroup $H_f$ of the product of the unit groups, there are a compact open product subgroup $U\\subset H_f$ and a smooth vector $\\varphi_U$ for which $$W_{\\varphi_U}(y)=\\mathbf{1}_U(y)\n \\qquad\\left(y\\in\\prod_{v\\in S_f}F_v^\\times\\right).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-029.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-whittaker-cutoff",
          "exact_quote": "Let $S_f$ be a finite set of nonarchimedean places and let $\\lambda_f\\ne0$ be a Whittaker functional with character $\\prod_{v\\in S_f}\\psi_v$ on a smooth representation of the product of the local covers. For a vector $\\varphi$, put $$W_\\varphi(y)=\\lambda_f\\!\\left(\\pi\\bigl((a(y_v))_{v\\in S_f}\\bigr)\n                         \\varphi\\right),\n \\qquad y\\in\\prod_{v\\in S_f}F_v^\\times.$$ Given any open subgroup $H_f$ of the product of the unit groups, there are a compact open product subgroup $U\\subset H_f$ and a smooth vector $\\varphi_U$ for which $$W_{\\varphi_U}(y)=\\mathbf{1}_U(y)\n \\qquad\\left(y\\in\\prod_{v\\in S_f}F_v^\\times\\right).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-029.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $S_f$ be a finite set of nonarchimedean places and let $\\lambda_f\\ne0$ be a Whittaker functional with character $\\prod_{v\\in S_f}\\psi_v$ on a smooth representation of the product of the local covers. For a vector $\\varphi$, put $$W_\\varphi(y)=\\lambda_f\\!\\left(\\pi\\bigl((a(y_v))_{v\\in S_f}\\bigr)\n                         \\varphi\\right),\n \\qquad y\\in\\prod_{v\\in S_f}F_v^\\times.$$ Given any open subgroup $H_f$ of the product of the unit groups, there are a compact open product subgroup $U\\subset H_f$ and a smooth vector $\\varphi_U$ for which $$W_{\\varphi_U}(y)=\\mathbf{1}_U(y)\n \\qquad\\left(y\\in\\prod_{v\\in S_f}F_v^\\times\\right).$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-sheet-profile",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal I$ be a finite index set. For every $\\kappa\\in\\mathcal I$, let $f_\\kappa$ be a finite sum of products $$f_\\kappa(y)=\\sum_{\\ell=1}^{L_\\kappa}\n                \\prod_{v\\in S}f_{\\kappa,\\ell,v}(y_v),\n \\qquad y\\in F_S^\\times.$$ Assume that for one $\\delta>0$ all these local factors satisfy the following conditions.*\n\n1.  *At a finite $v$, $f_{\\kappa,\\ell,v}$ is locally constant, vanishes for $|y_v|_v>C_v$, and is $O(|y_v|_v^\\delta)$ for $|y_v|_v\\le1$.*\n\n2.  *At a complex $v$, the factor is smooth on $F_v^\\times$ and, for every $j\\ge0$ and $A>0$, $$|\\mathcal D_v^j f_{\\kappa,\\ell,v}(y_v)|\n          \\le C_{A,j}\\min(|y_v|_v^\\delta,|y_v|_v^{-A}).$$*\n\n\n\n*All constants may depend on the finite family. Define $$H_\\kappa(u)=\\int_{X_u}f_\\kappa(y)\\,\\,\\mathrm{d}^\\times_u y,\\qquad\n D=u\\frac{\\,\\mathrm{d}}{\\,\\mathrm{d}u}.$$ Fix one complex place $v_0$, and define the nonnegative absolute derivative profile $$H^{\\mathrm{abs}}_{\\kappa,j}(u)\n =\\sum_{\\ell=1}^{L_\\kappa}\\int_{X_u}\n   |\\mathcal D_{v_0}^j f_{\\kappa,\\ell,v_0}(y_{v_0})|\n   \\prod_{v\\ne v_0}|f_{\\kappa,\\ell,v}(y_v)|\\,\\,\\mathrm{d}^\\times_u y.$$ Then $H_\\kappa$ is smooth, and for every $j\\ge0$ and $\\sigma>-\\delta$, $$\\label{eq:sheet-weighted-bound}\n |D^jH_\\kappa(u)|\\le H^{\\mathrm{abs}}_{\\kappa,j}(u)\n      \\le C_{\\sigma,j}u^{-\\sigma}\\qquad(u>0).$$ In particular, for any $0<\\alpha<\\delta$ and every $A>0$, $$\\label{eq:sheet-two-ended-bound}\n |D^jH_\\kappa(u)|+H^{\\mathrm{abs}}_{\\kappa,j}(u)\n    \\le C_{A,j}\\min(u^\\alpha,u^{-A}).$$ The conclusions are unchanged if each $f_\\kappa$ is multiplied by a fixed smooth unitary character of $F_S^\\times$ and by a bounded function of its class in the finite group $\\mathcal V$.*\n\n\n*Let $\\mathcal K:(0,\\infty)\\to\\mathbb{C}$ have every absolute moment $$M_\\beta(\\mathcal K)=\\int_0^\\infty|\\mathcal K(T)|T^\\beta\n                          \\,\\frac{\\,\\mathrm{d}T}{T}<\\infty\n \\qquad(\\beta\\in\\mathbb{R}).$$ Both convolutions $$\\int_0^\\infty\\mathcal K(T)H_\\kappa(uT)\\,\\frac{\\,\\mathrm{d}T}{T},\n \\qquad\n \\Phi_\\kappa(u):=\\int_0^\\infty\\mathcal K(T)H_\\kappa(u/T)\n                                      \\,\\frac{\\,\\mathrm{d}T}{T}$$ and their norm derivatives obey <a href=\"#eq:sheet-two-ended-bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:sheet-two-ended-bound\">[eq:sheet-two-ended-bound]</a>, with constants depending also on the indicated moments. For every $j$, the two absolute majorants $\\int|\\mathcal K(T)|H^{\\mathrm{abs}}_{\\kappa,j}(uT^{\\pm1})\\,\\,\\mathrm{d}T/T$ satisfy the same two-ended size bound. For any $x_i>0$, complex $a_i$, indices $\\kappa_i\\in\\mathcal I$, $\\tau\\in\\{1,-1\\}$, and $\\sigma>-\\delta$, one has the absolute bound $$\\label{eq:sheet-absolute-sum}\n\\begin{split}\n &\\int_0^\\infty|\\mathcal K(T)|\n       \\sum_i|a_i|H^{\\mathrm{abs}}_{\\kappa_i,0}(u x_iT^\\tau)\n                                     \\,\\frac{\\,\\mathrm{d}T}{T}\\\\\n &\\hspace{12mm}\\le\n C_{\\sigma,0}u^{-\\sigma}M_{-\\tau\\sigma}(\\mathcal K)\n       \\sum_i|a_i|x_i^{-\\sigma}.\n\\end{split}$$ In particular the sum and integral may be interchanged if the final series is finite.",
        "source_id": "PM-029.1"
      },

Define $$H_\\kappa(u)=\\int_{X_u}f_\\kappa(y)\\,\\,\\mathrm{d}^\\times_u y,\\qquad\n D=u\\frac{\\,\\mathrm{d}}{\\,\\mathrm{d}u}.$$ Fix one complex place $v_0$, and define the nonnegative absolute derivative profile $$H^{\\mathrm{abs}}_{\\kappa,j}(u)\n =\\sum_{\\ell=1}^{L_\\kappa}\\int_{X_u}\n   |\\mathcal D_{v_0}^j f_{\\kappa,\\ell,v_0}(y_{v_0})|\n   \\prod_{v\\ne v_0}|f_{\\kappa,\\ell,v}(y_v)|\\,\\,\\mathrm{d}^\\times_u y.$$ Then $H_\\kappa$ is smooth, and for every $j\\ge0$ and $\\sigma>-\\delta$, $$\\label{eq:sheet-weighted-bound}\n |D^jH_\\kappa(u)|\\le H^{\\mathrm{abs}}_{\\kappa,j}(u)\n      \\le C_{\\sigma,j}u^{-\\sigma}\\qquad(u>0).$$ In particular, for any $0<\\alpha<\\delta$ and every $A>0$, $$\\label{eq:sheet-two-ended-bound}\n |D^jH_\\kappa(u)|+H^{\\mathrm{abs}}_{\\kappa,j}(u)\n    \\le C_{A,j}\\min(u^\\alpha,u^{-A}).$$ The conclusions are unchanged if each $f_\\kappa$ is multiplied by a fixed smooth unitary character of $F_S^\\times$ and by a bounded function of its class in the finite group $\\mathcal V$.*\n\n\n*Let $\\mathcal K:(0,\\infty)\\to\\mathbb{C}$ have every absolute moment $$M_\\beta(\\mathcal K)=\\int_0^\\infty|\\mathcal K(T)|T^\\beta\n                          \\,\\frac{\\,\\mathrm{d}T}{T}<\\infty\n \\qquad(\\beta\\in\\mathbb{R}).$$ Both convolutions $$\\int_0^\\infty\\mathcal K(T)H_\\kappa(uT)\\,\\frac{\\,\\mathrm{d}T}{T},\n \\qquad\n \\Phi_\\kappa(u):=\\int_0^\\infty\\mathcal K(T)H_\\kappa(u/T)\n                                      \\,\\frac{\\,\\mathrm{d}T}{T}$$ and their norm derivatives obey <a href=\"#eq:sheet-two-ended-bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:sheet-two-ended-bound\">[eq:sheet-two-ended-bound]</a>, with constants depending also on the indicated moments.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-gaussian-smoothing",
        "exact_quote": "For $V$ in <a href=\"#eq:theta-V-profile\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:theta-V-profile\">[eq:theta-V-profile]</a>, there are constants $C_0>0$ and $A_0>0$ such that $$\\label{eq:theta-V-mellin}\n \\widehat V(s)\n =C_0 A_0^s\n       \\bigl[\\Gamma(s+5/6)\\Gamma(s+7/6)\\bigr]^r\n \\qquad(\\operatorname{Re}s>-5/6).$$ There is a smooth function $\\mathcal K:(0,\\infty)\\to\\mathbb{C}$ such that, for every $N>0$ and $j\\ge0$, $$\\label{eq:theta-kernel-bounds}\n \\left|(T\\partial_T)^j\\mathcal K(T)\\right|\n       \\le C_{N,j}\\min(T^N,T^{-N}),$$ and, for every $u>0$, $$\\label{eq:theta-exact-gaussian}\n \\int_0^\\infty\\mathcal K(T)V(uT)\\,\\frac{\\,\\mathrm{d}T}{T}\n  =P_G(u)=\\frac{1}{2\\sqrt{\\pi}}\n       \\exp\\!\\left(-\\frac{(\\log u)^2}{4}\\right).$$ Its Mellin transform satisfies the exact sign convention $$\\label{eq:theta-kernel-mellin}\n \\widehat{\\mathcal K}(-s)\n       =\\frac{e^{s^2}}{\\widehat V(s)}.$$ The quotient on the right is interpreted by its entire continuation. For every fixed finite family of rotated $S$-profiles $H_\\kappa$ covered by [lem:sheet-profile](#lem:sheet-profile), the functions $\\Phi_\\kappa(u)=\\int\\mathcal K(T)H_\\kappa(u/T)\\,\\,\\mathrm{d}T/T$ obey the signed and absolute bounds of that lemma.",
        "source_id": "PM-029.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-gaussian-smoothing",
          "exact_quote": "For $V$ in <a href=\"#eq:theta-V-profile\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:theta-V-profile\">[eq:theta-V-profile]</a>, there are constants $C_0>0$ and $A_0>0$ such that $$\\label{eq:theta-V-mellin}\n \\widehat V(s)\n =C_0 A_0^s\n       \\bigl[\\Gamma(s+5/6)\\Gamma(s+7/6)\\bigr]^r\n \\qquad(\\operatorname{Re}s>-5/6).$$ There is a smooth function $\\mathcal K:(0,\\infty)\\to\\mathbb{C}$ such that, for every $N>0$ and $j\\ge0$, $$\\label{eq:theta-kernel-bounds}\n \\left|(T\\partial_T)^j\\mathcal K(T)\\right|\n       \\le C_{N,j}\\min(T^N,T^{-N}),$$ and, for every $u>0$, $$\\label{eq:theta-exact-gaussian}\n \\int_0^\\infty\\mathcal K(T)V(uT)\\,\\frac{\\,\\mathrm{d}T}{T}\n  =P_G(u)=\\frac{1}{2\\sqrt{\\pi}}\n       \\exp\\!\\left(-\\frac{(\\log u)^2}{4}\\right).$$ Its Mellin transform satisfies the exact sign convention $$\\label{eq:theta-kernel-mellin}\n \\widehat{\\mathcal K}(-s)\n       =\\frac{e^{s^2}}{\\widehat V(s)}.$$ The quotient on the right is interpreted by its entire continuation. For every fixed finite family of rotated $S$-profiles $H_\\kappa$ covered by [lem:sheet-profile](#lem:sheet-profile), the functions $\\Phi_\\kappa(u)=\\int\\mathcal K(T)H_\\kappa(u/T)\\,\\,\\mathrm{d}T/T$ obey the signed and absolute bounds of that lemma.",
          "source_id": "PM-029.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For $V$ in <a href=\"#eq:theta-V-profile\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:theta-V-profile\">[eq:theta-V-profile]</a>, there are constants $C_0>0$ and $A_0>0$ such that $$\\label{eq:theta-V-mellin}\n \\widehat V(s)\n =C_0 A_0^s\n       \\bigl[\\Gamma(s+5/6)\\Gamma(s+7/6)\\bigr]^r\n \\qquad(\\operatorname{Re}s>-5/6).$$ There is a smooth function $\\mathcal K:(0,\\infty)\\to\\mathbb{C}$ such that, for every $N>0$ and $j\\ge0$, $$\\label{eq:theta-kernel-bounds}\n \\left|(T\\partial_T)^j\\mathcal K(T)\\right|\n       \\le C_{N,j}\\min(T^N,T^{-N}),$$ and, for every $u>0$, $$\\label{eq:theta-exact-gaussian}\n \\int_0^\\infty\\mathcal K(T)V(uT)\\,\\frac{\\,\\mathrm{d}T}{T}\n  =P_G(u)=\\frac{1}{2\\sqrt{\\pi}}\n       \\exp\\!\\left(-\\frac{(\\log u)^2}{4}\\right).$$ Its Mellin transform satisfies the exact sign convention $$\\label{eq:theta-kernel-mellin}\n \\widehat{\\mathcal K}(-s)\n       =\\frac{e^{s^2}}{\\widehat V(s)}.$$ The quotient on the right is interpreted by its entire continuation. For every fixed finite family of rotated $S$-profiles $H_\\kappa$ covered by [lem:sheet-profile](#lem:sheet-profile), the functions $\\Phi_\\kappa(u)=\\int\\mathcal K(T)H_\\kappa(u/T)\\,\\,\\mathrm{d}T/T$ obey the signed and absolute bounds of that lemma.",
      "explanation": "A rapidly decaying smooth kernel exists whose multiplicative convolution with the radial profile is exactly the Gaussian weight, via an explicit Mellin identity.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-reflection-theta-representation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V$ and $\\mathcal K$ be the common unrotated profile and Gaussian averaging weight from [lem:gaussian-smoothing](#lem:gaussian-smoothing). For $l,Z>0$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathcal T_m(l)\n &=\\sum_{\\substack{\\mathfrak c\\ \\mathrm{squarefree}\\\\\\mathfrak n}}\n    \\frac{\\gamma_2(\\mathfrak c)}{q_{\\mathfrak c}^{1/2}q_{\\mathfrak n}}\n    L(A)\\prod_{\\mathfrak p\\mid\\mathfrak m}\n                  \\chi_{\\mathfrak p}(A)^{j_{\\mathfrak p}}\n    V(q_{\\mathfrak A}l),                                      \\label{eq:reflection-unreflected}\\\\\n B_m(Z)\n &=\\int_0^\\infty\\mathcal K(T)\\mathcal T_m(T/Z)\\,\\frac{\\,\\mathrm{d}T}{T}.\n                                                               \\label{eq:reflection-exact-average}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $\\mathfrak A=\\mathfrak c\\mathfrak n^3$ and $A=cn^3$ is an allowed product generator. The ideal sums are over integral exterior ideals. Both formulas converge absolutely after the sheet integrations, and the second converges absolutely after the $T$-integration.",
        "source_id": "PM-029.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-reflection-theta-representation",
          "exact_quote": "Let $V$ and $\\mathcal K$ be the common unrotated profile and Gaussian averaging weight from [lem:gaussian-smoothing](#lem:gaussian-smoothing). For $l,Z>0$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathcal T_m(l)\n &=\\sum_{\\substack{\\mathfrak c\\ \\mathrm{squarefree}\\\\\\mathfrak n}}\n    \\frac{\\gamma_2(\\mathfrak c)}{q_{\\mathfrak c}^{1/2}q_{\\mathfrak n}}\n    L(A)\\prod_{\\mathfrak p\\mid\\mathfrak m}\n                  \\chi_{\\mathfrak p}(A)^{j_{\\mathfrak p}}\n    V(q_{\\mathfrak A}l),                                      \\label{eq:reflection-unreflected}\\\\\n B_m(Z)\n &=\\int_0^\\infty\\mathcal K(T)\\mathcal T_m(T/Z)\\,\\frac{\\,\\mathrm{d}T}{T}.\n                                                               \\label{eq:reflection-exact-average}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $\\mathfrak A=\\mathfrak c\\mathfrak n^3$ and $A=cn^3$ is an allowed product generator. The ideal sums are over integral exterior ideals. Both formulas converge absolutely after the sheet integrations, and the second converges absolutely after the $T$-integration.",
          "source_id": "PM-029.1"

      "exact_statement": "Let $V$ and $\\mathcal K$ be the common unrotated profile and Gaussian averaging weight from [lem:gaussian-smoothing](#lem:gaussian-smoothing). For $l,Z>0$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathcal T_m(l)\n &=\\sum_{\\substack{\\mathfrak c\\ \\mathrm{squarefree}\\\\\\mathfrak n}}\n    \\frac{\\gamma_2(\\mathfrak c)}{q_{\\mathfrak c}^{1/2}q_{\\mathfrak n}}\n    L(A)\\prod_{\\mathfrak p\\mid\\mathfrak m}\n                  \\chi_{\\mathfrak p}(A)^{j_{\\mathfrak p}}\n    V(q_{\\mathfrak A}l),                                      \\label{eq:reflection-unreflected}\\\\\n B_m(Z)\n &=\\int_0^\\infty\\mathcal K(T)\\mathcal T_m(T/Z)\\,\\frac{\\,\\mathrm{d}T}{T}.\n                                                               \\label{eq:reflection-exact-average}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $\\mathfrak A=\\mathfrak c\\mathfrak n^3$ and $A=cn^3$ is an allowed product generator. The ideal sums are over integral exterior ideals. Both formulas converge absolutely after the sheet integrations, and the second converges absolutely after the $T$-integration.",

      "id": "claim-reflection-theta-representation",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-reflection-local-weyl",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathfrak p$ be an exterior prime and put $W_e=W_{\\mathfrak p}(a(p^e))$. For $j\\in\\{0,\\ldots,5\\}$, a unit $t$, and an integer $e$, define $$\\mathcal R_{\\mathfrak p,j,e}(t)\n =\\sum_{v\\in k_{\\mathfrak p}^{\\times}}\\alpha_{\\mathfrak p,j}(v)\n       W_{\\mathfrak p}(a(p^{e-2}t)w n(v/p)).$$ It vanishes for $e<0$. For $e\\ge0$, $$\\label{eq:reflection-local-formula}\n \\mathcal R_{\\mathfrak p,j,e}(t)\n =W_e\\chi_{\\mathfrak p}(t)^{4+2e}\n   \\sum_{v\\in k_{\\mathfrak p}^{\\times}}\n     \\alpha_{\\mathfrak p,j}(v)\\chi_{\\mathfrak p}(v)^4\n     \\psi_{\\mathfrak p}(p^{e-1}t/v).$$ For $j=1$, it vanishes for $e>0$, while $$\\label{eq:reflection-valuation-one}\n\\begin{split}\n \\mathcal R_{\\mathfrak p,1,0}(t)\n      &=\\omega_{\\mathfrak p}\\chi_{\\mathfrak p}(t),\n       \\qquad |\\omega_{\\mathfrak p}|=1,\\\\\n \\omega_{\\mathfrak p}\n &=\\frac{\\sum_{x\\ne0}\\chi_{\\mathfrak p}(x)^3\\psi_{\\mathfrak p}(x/p)}\n        {\\tau_{\\mathfrak p,1}}.\n\\end{split}$$ For all $j$, its magnitude at $e=0$ is at most $1$, and for $e>0$ is at most $q_{\\mathfrak p}^{1/2}|W_e|$. More precisely, for $e\\ge0$ put $s_{\\mathfrak p,j,e}=\\mathcal R_{\\mathfrak p,j,e}(1)$ and choose the exponents modulo $6$ by $$\\label{eq:reflection-local-monomial}\n b_{j,0}\\equiv8-j,\\qquad b_{j,e}\\equiv4+2e\\ (e>0),\\qquad\n \\mathcal R_{\\mathfrak p,j,e}(t)\n       =s_{\\mathfrak p,j,e}\\chi_{\\mathfrak p}(t)^{b_{j,e}}.$$ For $e>0$, $s_{\\mathfrak p,j,e}=0$ unless $j=4$, and it also vanishes for $e\\equiv2\\pmod3$. The zero-digit term is available only for $j=0$; at a frequency $\\nu$ it is $(1-q_{\\mathfrak p}^{-1})W_{\\mathfrak p}(a(\\nu))$, with ordinary spherical support.",
        "source_id": "PM-029.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-reflection-local-weyl",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathfrak p$ be an exterior prime and put $W_e=W_{\\mathfrak p}(a(p^e))$. For $j\\in\\{0,\\ldots,5\\}$, a unit $t$, and an integer $e$, define $$\\mathcal R_{\\mathfrak p,j,e}(t)\n =\\sum_{v\\in k_{\\mathfrak p}^{\\times}}\\alpha_{\\mathfrak p,j}(v)\n       W_{\\mathfrak p}(a(p^{e-2}t)w n(v/p)).$$ It vanishes for $e<0$. For $e\\ge0$, $$\\label{eq:reflection-local-formula}\n \\mathcal R_{\\mathfrak p,j,e}(t)\n =W_e\\chi_{\\mathfrak p}(t)^{4+2e}\n   \\sum_{v\\in k_{\\mathfrak p}^{\\times}}\n     \\alpha_{\\mathfrak p,j}(v)\\chi_{\\mathfrak p}(v)^4\n     \\psi_{\\mathfrak p}(p^{e-1}t/v).$$ For $j=1$, it vanishes for $e>0$, while $$\\label{eq:reflection-valuation-one}\n\\begin{split}\n \\mathcal R_{\\mathfrak p,1,0}(t)\n      &=\\omega_{\\mathfrak p}\\chi_{\\mathfrak p}(t),\n       \\qquad |\\omega_{\\mathfrak p}|=1,\\\\\n \\omega_{\\mathfrak p}\n &=\\frac{\\sum_{x\\ne0}\\chi_{\\mathfrak p}(x)^3\\psi_{\\mathfrak p}(x/p)}\n        {\\tau_{\\mathfrak p,1}}.\n\\end{split}$$ For all $j$, its magnitude at $e=0$ is at most $1$, and for $e>0$ is at most $q_{\\mathfrak p}^{1/2}|W_e|$. More precisely, for $e\\ge0$ put $s_{\\mathfrak p,j,e}=\\mathcal R_{\\mathfrak p,j,e}(1)$ and choose the exponents modulo $6$ by $$\\label{eq:reflection-local-monomial}\n b_{j,0}\\equiv8-j,\\qquad b_{j,e}\\equiv4+2e\\ (e>0),\\qquad\n \\mathcal R_{\\mathfrak p,j,e}(t)\n       =s_{\\mathfrak p,j,e}\\chi_{\\mathfrak p}(t)^{b_{j,e}}.$$ For $e>0$, $s_{\\mathfrak p,j,e}=0$ unless $j=4$, and it also vanishes for $e\\equiv2\\pmod3$. The zero-digit term is available only for $j=0$; at a frequency $\\nu$ it is $(1-q_{\\mathfrak p}^{-1})W_{\\mathfrak p}(a(\\nu))$, with ordinary spherical support.",
          "source_id": "PM-029.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $\\mathfrak p$ be an exterior prime and put $W_e=W_{\\mathfrak p}(a(p^e))$. For $j\\in\\{0,\\ldots,5\\}$, a unit $t$, and an integer $e$, define $$\\mathcal R_{\\mathfrak p,j,e}(t)\n =\\sum_{v\\in k_{\\mathfrak p}^{\\times}}\\alpha_{\\mathfrak p,j}(v)\n       W_{\\mathfrak p}(a(p^{e-2}t)w n(v/p)).$$ It vanishes for $e<0$. For $e\\ge0$, $$\\label{eq:reflection-local-formula}\n \\mathcal R_{\\mathfrak p,j,e}(t)\n =W_e\\chi_{\\mathfrak p}(t)^{4+2e}\n   \\sum_{v\\in k_{\\mathfrak p}^{\\times}}\n     \\alpha_{\\mathfrak p,j}(v)\\chi_{\\mathfrak p}(v)^4\n     \\psi_{\\mathfrak p}(p^{e-1}t/v).$$ For $j=1$, it vanishes for $e>0$, while $$\\label{eq:reflection-valuation-one}\n\\begin{split}\n \\mathcal R_{\\mathfrak p,1,0}(t)\n      &=\\omega_{\\mathfrak p}\\chi_{\\mathfrak p}(t),\n       \\qquad |\\omega_{\\mathfrak p}|=1,\\\\\n \\omega_{\\mathfrak p}\n &=\\frac{\\sum_{x\\ne0}\\chi_{\\mathfrak p}(x)^3\\psi_{\\mathfrak p}(x/p)}\n        {\\tau_{\\mathfrak p,1}}.\n\\end{split}$$ For all $j$, its magnitude at $e=0$ is at most $1$, and for $e>0$ is at most $q_{\\mathfrak p}^{1/2}|W_e|$. More precisely, for $e\\ge0$ put $s_{\\mathfrak p,j,e}=\\mathcal R_{\\mathfrak p,j,e}(1)$ and choose the exponents modulo $6$ by $$\\label{eq:reflection-local-monomial}\n b_{j,0}\\equiv8-j,\\qquad b_{j,e}\\equiv4+2e\\ (e>0),\\qquad\n \\mathcal R_{\\mathfrak p,j,e}(t)\n       =s_{\\mathfrak p,j,e}\\chi_{\\mathfrak p}(t)^{b_{j,e}}.$$ For $e>0$, $s_{\\mathfrak p,j,e}=0$ unless $j=4$, and it also vanishes for $e\\equiv2\\pmod3$. The zero-digit term is available only for $j=0$; at a frequency $\\nu$ it is $(1-q_{\\mathfrak p}^{-1})W_{\\mathfrak p}(a(\\nu))$, with ordinary spherical support.",
